<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Bloch's <i>T</i><sup>3/2</sup> law for the spontaneous magnetization of three-dimensional quantum Heisenberg ferromagnets at every fixed positive quantum spin. The result holds for every nonnegative symmetric finite-range interaction whose support generates ℤ<sup>3</sup>, including spatially anisotropic couplings. The leading magnetization deficit has the exact coefficient determined by the determinant of the quadratic one-magnon dispersion. The thermodynamic limit is taken before the right field derivative at zero, and the low-temperature limit is taken last.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed $S\in\{\tfrac12,1,\tfrac32,\ldots\}$ and every interaction satisfying <a href="#eq:interaction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:interaction">[eq:interaction]</a>, $$\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\beta^{3/2}\bigl(S-m_{S,J}(\beta)\bigr) =\frac{\zeta(3/2)}{8\pi^{3/2}\sqrt{\det D_{S,J}}}. \label{eq:bloch-law}$$
  - Explanation: The spontaneous magnetization deficit has the Bloch asymptotic coefficient determined by the full quadratic dispersion.
- **C2** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-loop-representation)): For every finite cube, every $\beta>0$, and every real $h$, $$\label{eq:loop-partition} Z_\Lambda(h):=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-\beta(H^J_{S,N}-hM_{S,N})} =e^{\beta w_\Lambda/4}\, \mathbb E_{\rm b}\sum_\sigma e^{\beta hM(\sigma)}.$$ At zero field the Gibbs spectral law of total magnetization is obtained by weighting each picture by $2^{\#\{\text{cycles}\}}$, coloring its cycles independently and fairly, and evaluating $M(\sigma)$. More precisely, for every function $\Phi$ on the finite spectrum of $M_{S,N}$, $$\label{eq:loop-spectral-law} \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\bigl(e^{-\beta H^J_{S,N}}\Phi(M_{S,N})\bigr)}{Z_\Lambda(0)} =\frac{\mathbb E_{\rm b}\sum_\sigma\Phi(M(\sigma))} {\mathbb E_{\rm b}2^{\#\{\text{cycles}\}}}.$$
  - Explanation: Poisson exchanges and uniform seam completions represent the quantum partition function and magnetization spectral law.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-dense-boundary)): Let $F$ be a finite deterministic pin set and let $\mathcal T_n$ be increasing finite sets of times containing zero, with dense union. Set $$D_n=F\cup\bigl(\partial\Lambda\times\{1,\ldots,\ell\} \times\mathcal T_n\bigr), \qquad D=F\cup D_0.$$ Almost surely, $n_{D_n}=n_D$ and $\mathcal B_{D_n}=\mathcal B_D$ for all sufficiently large $n$. Moreover $\mathbb P_{D_n}$ converges to $\mathbb P_D$ in total variation. If bounded observables $X_n$ converge almost surely to $X$, with a common deterministic bound, then $\mathbb E_{D_n}X_n\to\mathbb E_DX$.
  - Explanation: Dense finite boundary cuts eventually detect all boundary visits at fixed volume and converge in total variation.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-factorial)): For an allowed or finite pin set $D$, an integer $j\ge1$, and a nonnegative measurable function $F$ on $X_\Lambda^j$, $$\label{eq:factorial-bound} \mathbb E_D\sum_{(u_1,\ldots,u_j)\in(\omega_{\rm m})^j_{\ne}} F(u_1,\ldots,u_j) \le 2^j\int F\,d\lambda_{\rm b}^{\otimes j}.$$ Here the sum is over ordered tuples of distinct marks. The estimate also holds with any prescribed time order or time restrictions.
  - Explanation: Pinning permits uniform factorial domination of distinct exchange marks by the base intensity.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-pin-identities)): For $E,T$ as above, $$\label{eq:pin-identities} d_E(i)=\frac{r(E,\{i\})}{1+r(E,\{i\})}\quad(i\notin E), \qquad \mathbb P_E(T\subseteq\mathcal B_E)\le(2r(E,T))^{m_T}.$$
  - Explanation: A singleton return fraction determines the down probability, while simultaneous avoidance obeys a negative dependence bound.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-exposure)): Almost surely under $\mathbb P_P$, $g_P(\mathbf a)>0$ and the conditional remaining-picture law given $\mathscr H$ is $\mathbb Q_{\mathbf a}(\,\cdot\mid G_P)$.* *More precisely, for a deterministic initial label set $A\subseteq V_\Lambda$, let $\mu_A$ be the base distribution of its trajectories and endpoint mappings. Let $F_A$ be the event that these paths avoid $P$ and their endpoint set is $A$. On $F_A$, let $c_A(\mathbf a)$ be the number of cycles of their endpoint permutation. For any bounded measurable $\Phi$ of the exposure and remaining picture $\omega_{\rm h}$, $$\label{eq:exposure-disintegration} \mathbb E_P\Phi(\mathbf a,\omega_{\rm h}) =\frac1{\mathcal Z_P}\sum_{A\subseteq V_\Lambda} \int_{F_A}2^{c_A(\mathbf a)} \mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_{\mathbf a}} [\mathbf 1_{G_P}\Phi(\mathbf a,\omega_{\rm h})] \,\mu_A(d\mathbf a).$$ In particular, the exposure marginal on this branch is $$\label{eq:exposure-marginal} \mathcal Z_P^{-1}\mathbf 1_{F_A}2^{c_A(\mathbf a)}g_P(\mathbf a) \,\mu_A(d\mathbf a).$$ The same assertions hold for finite deterministic $P$.
  - Explanation: Exposing all cycles missing the pins leaves the remaining picture conditioned on every remaining cycle meeting the pins.
- **C7** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-exterior-moments)): Suppose a random permutation matrix $\Pi$ on a finite set is a distributional limit of products of independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations. If $K=\mathbb E\Pi$, then $$\label{eq:exterior-moments} \mathbb E\bigwedge\nolimits^k\Pi=\bigwedge\nolimits^kK \qquad(0\le k\le\|V\|).$$ For every fixed exposure and finite set of cuts, the layered permutation obtained under $\mathbb Q_{\mathbf a}$ by following holes to the next cyclic cut has this property.
  - Explanation: Independent Bernoulli transpositions preserve the exterior-moment identity, including its distributional limits.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-next-pin-kernel)): Let $V=I\sqcup B$ be a finite set with $I\ne\varnothing$. Suppose a random permutation matrix $\Pi$ satisfies <a href="#eq:exterior-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exterior-moments">[eq:exterior-moments]</a>, and let $K=\mathbb E\Pi$. Let $G_I$ be the event that every cycle meets $I$. On $G_I$, let $\Sigma$ be the column-forward permutation matrix on $I$ giving the next positive visit to $I$. Then $$\label{eq:all-hit-determinant} \Pr(G_I)=\det(I-K_{BB}).$$ If this probability is positive, then $K_{BB}$ is transient and $$\label{eq:conditional-next-pin} \mathbb E[\Sigma\mid G_I] =K_{II}+K_{IB}(I-K_{BB})^{-1}K_{BI}.$$ The right-hand side is the first positive $I$-hitting kernel of the Markov chain with independent transitions according to $K$. If $B$ is empty, the determinant is one and the correction is zero.
  - Explanation: The all-cycle hitting probability is a determinant, and the conditional next-pin first moment equals the fresh Markov hitting kernel.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-fresh-walk)): For almost every exposure under $\mathbb P_P$ and every $i\in T$, $$\mathbb P_P(\mathcal R_i(E,T)\mid\mathscr H)=q_i(\mathbf a).$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:fresh-return} m_Tr(E,T)=\mathbb E_P\sum_{i\in T}q_i(\mathbf a).$$
  - Explanation: Conditional next-pin return probabilities are represented by a walk with fresh transitions in a repeated exposure.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-nash)): Suppose $R\ge R_0$ and $t$ is regular for $z$ at radius $R$. Every nonnegative function $v$ on $\mathcal H_t$, supported on slots above $\mathfrak B_R(z)$, satisfies $$\label{eq:local-nash} \\|v\\|_2^{10/3}\le C\mathcal D_t(v)\\|v\\|_1^{4/3}.$$ The constant is independent of $R,z,t$ and the unavailable slots.
  - Explanation: Local regularity yields a Nash inequality on available slots with a uniform geometric constant.
- **C11** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-routed-flow)): Consider an available-slot nearest-neighbor graph on $\mathbb Z^3$, with at most $\ell$ slots per site, all pairs of available slots above each nearest-neighbor site edge joined, and all slots available outside a finite set. Suppose $z$ satisfies the two conditions in Definition <a href="#def:regular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:regular">11</a> for this graph, and $R\ge R_0$. For each available slot $a$ at $y\in B_R(z)$, there is a flow $F_a$ such that $$\label{eq:global-flow-envelope} \mathop{\mathrm{div}}F_a=\mathbf 1_{\{a\}},\qquad \|F_a(e)\|\le C(1+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(e,y))^{-k},\qquad k=2-3\nu=\frac{199}{100}.$$ The flows can be chosen with the following common routing construction. For each blocked component $C$, thicken it by supremum radius four, fill the finite nearest-neighbor components of the complement, and call the resulting finite hull $H_C$. Let $\operatorname{Box}_C^+$ be the unit enlargement of the coordinate bounding box of that thickening. The hull lies in this box. Keep the inclusion-maximal hulls, which are disjoint, and send each original site in such a hull to a chosen surviving exterior-boundary representative, fixing other sites. Apply this map only once to each original endpoint.* *There are simple surviving routes for the original lattice edges. Each route is contained in its original edge together with the boxes $\operatorname{Box}_C^+$ of the maximal hulls containing its original endpoints. Lift the routes using a fixed available slot at every surviving site. The flow $F_a$ is the sum of a unit path flow along a connector of uniformly bounded length and displacement from $a$, and the routed values of a lattice unit flow $G_y$ satisfying $$\label{eq:lattice-flow-envelope} \|G_y(e')\|\le C(1+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(e',y))^{-2}.$$ All constants are uniform over the graph, anchor, radius, and source.
  - Explanation: Routed lattice unit flows avoid blocked hulls and retain a uniform decay envelope.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-sparse-energy)): Let $R\ge R_0$, and let $(z_i)_{i=1}^m$ be a sparse list of centers, counting repetitions. At a fixed slice let $I\subseteq \{1,\ldots,m\}$ be any subset for which the slice is regular for $z_i$ at radius $R$. For $i\in I$, let $\mu_i\ge0$ be a mass on the available slots, supported above $\mathfrak B_R(z_i)$ and with total mass at most one. For every finitely supported real $Y$, $$\label{eq:sparse-source-pairing} \left\|\left\langle\sum_{i\in I}\mu_i,Y\right\rangle\right\|^2 \le C m R^\alpha\mathcal D_t(Y).$$
  - Explanation: Sparse source masses have an energy pairing bound uniform in the retained regular centers.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-near-flow)): The same flows as in Lemma <a href="#lem:routed-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:routed-flow">13</a> satisfy $$\label{eq:near-flow-envelope} \|F_a(e)\|\le C(1+h)^{-2} \qquad\text{if }h=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(e,y)\le R.$$ The constant and the lower threshold $R_0$ are independent of $R$.
  - Explanation: Near a regular source, the routed flows retain inverse-square decay.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-spread-energy)): Suppose $R\ge R_0$, $1\le\tau\le R$, and the slice $t$ is regular for $z$ at radius $R$. Let $\mu\ge0$ be supported on available slots above $\mathfrak B_R(z)$, with $$\\|\mu\\|_1\le1,\qquad \\|\mu\\|_2\le C_1\tau^{-3/4}.$$ For every finitely supported real function $Y$ on $\mathcal H_t$, $$\label{eq:spread-source-pairing} \|\langle\mu,Y\rangle\|^2 \le C\tau^{-1/2}\mathcal D_t(Y).$$ The constant may depend on $C_1$ and the fixed geometry, but is independent of $\tau,R$, the slice, and the volume.
  - Explanation: Diffused subunit mass has a sharper energy pairing bound independent of the localization radius.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-blocking)): Assume $\mathsf I(P)$. At every deterministic time $t$, in every coset, a prescribed set $A$ of distinct coordinate sites satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P_P(A\subseteq\mathcal O_t^\gamma)&\le Cp^6 &&\text{if }\|A\|=6,\label{eq:six-blocking}\\ \mathbb P_P(A\subseteq\mathcal O_t^\gamma)&\le(C\sqrt p)^{\|A\|} &&\text{if }A\ne\varnothing\text{ is sparse}. \label{eq:sparse-blocking} \end{aligned}$$ The constants are uniform in the choice and size of the grid.
  - Explanation: Factorial mark domination transfers sparse blocking bounds from grid times to deterministic times.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-component-tail)): There are $\beta_0,c,C,\lambda>0$ such that, if $\beta\ge\beta_0$ and $\mathsf I(P)$ holds, then $$\label{eq:component-tail} \mathbb P_P\bigl(w\in\mathcal O_t^\gamma, \operatorname{rad}_w\mathcal C_t^\gamma(w)\ge h\bigr) \le C\exp(-ch^\lambda),\qquad h\ge1.$$ This holds for every deterministic time $t$, coset $\gamma$, and coordinate site $w$, with the same constants for all $\beta\ge\beta_0$.
  - Explanation: Separated sparse binary witnesses give a stretched-exponential blocked-component radius tail.
- **C17** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-irregularity)): Under $\mathsf I(P)$, for $\beta\ge\beta_0$, every deterministic time $t$, anchor $z\in\mathbb Z^3$, and radius $R\ge1$ satisfy $$\label{eq:irregularity} \mathbb P_P(t\text{ is not regular for }z\text{ at radius }R) \le\delta_R:=CR^3p^6+C\exp(-cR^\xi), \qquad \xi=\nu\lambda>0.$$
  - Explanation: Local blocking and remote component tails bound the probability that a deterministic slice is irregular.
- **C18** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-localization)): One may choose $a$ in <a href="#eq:scales" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scales">[eq:scales]</a> independently of $K$ so that the following holds, without assuming $\mathsf I(P)$. Let $i$ be a deterministic slot point that is almost surely a hole under the exposure law for an allowed pin set $P$, and put $z=\bar x_i$. Let $\mathcal K$ be a deterministic schedule of at most $2K+2$ full or partial boundary-killed traversals starting at $i$, each in either direction and each covering at most one period. Use matching layers at every concatenation. If $\mathcal K_R$ is localized throughout at $z$, then $$\label{eq:localization-loss} \mathbb E_P\bigl\\|(\mathcal K-\mathcal K_R) \mathbf 1_{\{i\}}\bigr\\|_1\le C_K e^{-c\beta}.$$ The same estimate holds for localization of the walk on the full slot lattice, with all slots available and no exposed trajectories.
  - Explanation: The factorial path count gives an exponentially small expected localization loss without the pin induction hypothesis.
- **C19** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-diffusion)): Fix an exposure good for $z,R_K$. Let a kernel be a subinterval of a concatenation of full traversals in arbitrary directions, localized throughout at $z$. For every nonnegative input of mass at most one, its output after elapsed time $b'\ge\beta/16$ satisfies $$\label{eq:diffusion-l2} \\|v_{\rm out}\\|_2\le C(b')^{-3/4}.$$ The same statement holds for the adjoint of any such subinterval. For such a kernel $\mathcal K_R$ of length $b'\ge\beta/8$, $$\label{eq:diffusion-entry} \max_{j,k}(\mathcal K_R)_{jk}\le C(b')^{-3/2}.$$ The constants are independent of $K$ and $R_K$.
  - Explanation: Nash dissipation during regular times gives uniform norm and entry decay on a good exposure.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-hitting-energy)): Let $E$ be allowed and let $T\ne\varnothing$ be a finite set of slot points at a deterministic cut $\theta$, disjoint from $E$. Put $P=E\cup T$, $m_T=\|T\|$, and $\eta=\mathbf 1_{T}$. At almost every exposure under $\mathbb P_P$, define $f=1$ on $T$; at every other cut hole let $f$ be the probability that the boundary-killed fresh walk ever hits $T$ at a subsequent integer turn. Then $0\le f\le1$, $f$ has zero boundary values, and, for $g=f-\eta$, $$\label{eq:hitting-energy} \sum_{i\in T}q_i(\mathbf a) \le m_T-\langle f,A_Qf\rangle =\langle\eta,Q\eta\rangle+2\langle Q\eta,g\rangle -\langle g,A_Qg\rangle.$$
  - Explanation: The finite-target fresh return is bounded by the hitting function and its symmetric energy.
- **C21** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-suffix-energy)): With these definitions and zero extension to the available-slot graph at the split, $$\label{eq:suffix-energy} \int_I\mathcal D_{t_d(u)}(Y_d(u))\,du \le\frac12\bigl(\\|y\\|_2^2-\\|U_d^*y\\|_2^2\bigr) \le E_y.$$ Moreover $\\|Y_d(u)\\|_\infty\le\\|y\\|_\infty$.
  - Explanation: The suffix energy integral is bounded by norm dissipation and the original quadratic energy.
- **C22** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-sparse-pins)): There is $\beta_1<\infty$, independent of the cube and pins, such that for every $\beta\ge\beta_1$, every allowed pin set $E$, and every nonempty sparse set $T$ at one deterministic time, disjoint from $E$, $$\label{eq:sparse-pins} \mathbb P_E(T\subseteq\mathcal B_E) \le\beta^{-9\|T\|/10}.$$ Consequently <a href="#eq:irregularity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:irregularity">[eq:irregularity]</a> and <a href="#eq:good-exposure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:good-exposure">[eq:good-exposure]</a> hold under every allowed pin law, with no induction hypothesis.
  - Explanation: Downward induction on extra pins proves uniform sparse avoidance and removes the induction hypothesis from regularity estimates.
- **C23** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-return-expansion)): For almost every exposure, define $$v=\sum_{n=1}^{K-1}Q^n\eta,\qquad y=g-v,\qquad E_y=\langle y,A_Qy\rangle,$$ with the empty sum interpreted as zero. Then $E_y\ge0$, $\\|y\\|_\infty\le K+1$, and the fresh return probability satisfies $$\label{eq:return-expansion} q_i\le\sum_{n=1}^{2K-1}\langle\eta,Q^n\eta\rangle +2\langle Q^K\eta,y\rangle-E_y .$$
  - Explanation: A finite telescoping expansion separates explicit return entries from an energy remainder.
- **C24** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-long-tail)): For every fixed integer $K\ge1$, as $\beta\to\infty$, $$\label{eq:long-tail} \mathbb E_P\bigl[2\langle Q^K\eta,y\rangle-E_y\bigr] \le C K^{-1/2}\beta^{-3/2}+o_K(\beta^{-3/2}).$$ Here $C$ depends only on the fixed interaction, spin, and geometric choices, and is independent of $K$. The error has the uniformity specified above.
  - Explanation: Spread-source energy bounds the long-return remainder with a leading constant independent of the truncation parameter.
- **C25** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-singleton-bound)): For every sufficiently large $\beta$, uniformly in all allowed pin sets $E$, cubes, cuts, and slot-time points $i\notin E$, $$\label{eq:singleton-return} r(E,\{i\})\le C\beta^{-3/2}.$$ Consequently, for every allowed pin set $D$ and every slot-time point $j$ at a deterministic time, $$\label{eq:free-slot} \mathbb P_D(j\in\mathcal B_D)\le C\beta^{-3/2}.$$
  - Explanation: The closed bootstrap and long-return estimate give sharp uniform singleton return and exposure bounds.
- **C26** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-ideal-kernel)): The full site kernel satisfies $$\label{eq:ideal-site-bounds} \sup_{y,w}p_t(y,w)\le C(1+t)^{-3/2},\qquad p_t(x,x)=t^{-3/2}(\kappa+o(1)).$$ The first estimate holds for all $t\ge0$; the second is as $t\to\infty$. For every fixed $K$, every $1\le n\le2K$, and every direction word of length $n$, $$\label{eq:ideal-diagonal} (\widehat W_\infty(\mathbf d))_{ii} =\ell^{-1}p_{n\beta}(x,x) =\beta^{-3/2}\bigl((\kappa/\ell)n^{-3/2}+o_K(1)\bigr).$$ The error is uniform in the word, cut, and initial slot. Always, $$\label{eq:ideal-killed-upper} 0\le(\widehat W_R(\mathbf d))_{ii} \le(\widehat W_\infty(\mathbf d))_{ii}.$$ If $\mathfrak B_R(z)\subseteq\Lambda^\circ$, then $$\label{eq:ideal-killed-interior} 0\le(\widehat W_\infty(\mathbf d))_{ii} -(\widehat W_R(\mathbf d))_{ii} \le C_K e^{-c\beta}.$$
  - Explanation: The full interaction dispersion determines the ideal heat-kernel coefficient and interior localization error.
- **C27** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-word)): For every fixed $K$, $$\label{eq:finite-word} \left\|\mathbb E_P[(W_R(\mathbf d))_{ii}] -(\widehat W_R(\mathbf d))_{ii}\right\| =o_K(\beta^{-3/2}).$$ The error is uniform over $1\le n\le2K$, all direction words of length $n$, all cubes, deterministic cuts and slots, and all allowed pin sets $E$ with arbitrary finitely many extra pins. The product inside the expectation is formed before averaging over the exposure.
  - Explanation: Midpoint smoothing and path coupling compare exposed and ideal returns in the same exposure.
- **C28** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-return-bounds)): There is a constant $C<\infty$, independent of $K$, with the following property. For every integer $K\ge1$ there are $\beta_K<\infty$ and a nonnegative function $\epsilon_K(\beta)\to0$ as $\beta\to\infty$, such that for $\beta\ge\beta_K$, every finite cube, every allowed pin set $E$, and every deterministic slot-time point $i$, $$\label{eq:down-upper} d_E(i)\le\beta^{-3/2} \left(\frac\kappa\ell\sum_{n=1}^{2K-1}n^{-3/2} +CK^{-1/2}+\epsilon_K(\beta)\right).$$ If the site $x$ of $i$ additionally satisfies $$\label{eq:pin-free-ball} \mathfrak B_{R_K}(\bar x)\subseteq\Lambda^\circ, \qquad E\text{ has no pin over }\mathfrak B_{R_K}(\bar x),$$ then $$\label{eq:down-lower} d_E(i)\ge\beta^{-3/2} \left(\frac\kappa\ell\sum_{n=1}^{K}n^{-3/2} -\epsilon_K(\beta)\right).$$ For $i\notin E$, the same two bounds hold for $r(E,\{i\})$, with the same choice of error function. The threshold and error function are independent of the cube, cut, slot, and number, locations, and times of all finite extra pins. Only the fixed model and geometric choices, and $K$, may affect them.
  - Explanation: Uniform sharp upper bounds and pin-free interior lower bounds determine single-slot return and down probabilities.
- **C29** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-pressure-equivalence)): For every fixed $\beta>0$ and $h\ge0$, $$\lim_{N\to\infty}p_N^+(h)=p_\beta(h). \label{eq:boundary-pressure}$$
  - Explanation: The boundary pin cost vanishes per volume, giving the same thermodynamic pressure.
- **C30** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-pin-mixture)): For $h\ge0$, the colored-picture law defining $Z^+_\Lambda(h)$ is a mixture of the zero-field pinned laws with pin sets $E=D_0\cup F$, where $F$ consists of time-zero slots. Under the mixing law, $$\Pr(i\in F)\le b(h):=1-e^{-\beta h} \qquad\text{for every time-zero slot }i. \label{eq:pin-marginal}$$ Moreover, $$S-(p_N^+)'(h) =\frac1V\sum_{i\in V_\Lambda}\mathbb E_F[d_{D_0\cup F}(i)]. \label{eq:field-deficit}$$
  - Explanation: A positive magnetic field is an exact mixture of zero-field extra-pin laws with controlled selection marginals.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-exposure",
        "exact_quote": "Almost surely under $\\mathbb P_P$, $g_P(\\mathbf a)>0$ and the conditional remaining-picture law given $\\mathscr H$ is $\\mathbb Q_{\\mathbf a}(\\,\\cdot\\mid G_P)$.*\n\n\n\n*More precisely, for a deterministic initial label set $A\\subseteq V_\\Lambda$, let $\\mu_A$ be the base distribution of its trajectories and endpoint mappings. Let $F_A$ be the event that these paths avoid $P$ and their endpoint set is $A$. On $F_A$, let $c_A(\\mathbf a)$ be the number of cycles of their endpoint permutation. For any bounded measurable $\\Phi$ of the exposure and remaining picture $\\omega_{\\rm h}$, $$\\label{eq:exposure-disintegration}\n \\mathbb E_P\\Phi(\\mathbf a,\\omega_{\\rm h})\n =\\frac1{\\mathcal Z_P}\\sum_{A\\subseteq V_\\Lambda}\n     \\int_{F_A}2^{c_A(\\mathbf a)}\n        \\mathbb E_{\\mathbb Q_{\\mathbf a}}\n                 [\\mathbf 1_{G_P}\\Phi(\\mathbf a,\\omega_{\\rm h})]\n                                            \\,\\mu_A(d\\mathbf a).$$ In particular, the exposure marginal on this branch is $$\\label{eq:exposure-marginal}\n \\mathcal Z_P^{-1}\\mathbf 1_{F_A}2^{c_A(\\mathbf a)}g_P(\\mathbf a)\n                                                   \\,\\mu_A(d\\mathbf a).$$ The same assertions hold for finite deterministic $P$.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Suppose a random permutation matrix $\\Pi$ on a finite set is a distributional limit of products of independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations.         }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-next-pin-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V=I\\sqcup B$ be a finite set with $I\\ne\\varnothing$. Suppose a random permutation matrix $\\Pi$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:exterior-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-moments\">[eq:exterior-moments]</a>, and let $K=\\mathbb E\\Pi$. Let $G_I$ be the event that every cycle meets $I$. On $G_I$, let $\\Sigma$ be the column-forward permutation matrix on $I$ giving the next positive visit to $I$. Then $$\\label{eq:all-hit-determinant}\n                         \\Pr(G_I)=\\det(I-K_{BB}).$$ If this probability is positive, then $K_{BB}$ is transient and $$\\label{eq:conditional-next-pin}\n \\mathbb E[\\Sigma\\mid G_I]\n       =K_{II}+K_{IB}(I-K_{BB})^{-1}K_{BI}.$$ The right-hand side is the first positive $I$-hitting kernel of the Markov chain with independent transitions according to $K$. If $B$ is empty, the determinant is one and the correction is zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-next-pin-kernel",

Let $G_I$ be the event that every cycle meets $I$. On $G_I$, let $\\Sigma$ be the column-forward permutation matrix on $I$ giving the next positive visit to $I$. Then $$\\label{eq:all-hit-determinant}\n                         \\Pr(G_I)=\\det(I-K_{BB}).$$ If this probability is positive, then $K_{BB}$ is transient and $$\\label{eq:conditional-next-pin}\n \\mathbb E[\\Sigma\\mid G_I]\n       =K_{II}+K_{IB}(I-K_{BB})^{-1}K_{BI}.$$ The right-hand side is the first positive $I$-hitting kernel of the Markov chain with independent transitions according to $K$. If $B$ is empty, the determinant is one and the correction is zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $V=I\\sqcup B$ be a finite set with $I\\ne\\varnothing$. Let $G_I$ be the event that every cycle meets $I$. On $G_I$, let $\\Sigma$ be the column-forward permutation matrix on $I$ giving the next positive visit to $I$. Let $G_I$ be the event that every cycle meets $I$. On $G_I$, let $\\Sigma$ be the column-forward permutation matrix on $I$ giving the next positive visit to $I$. Then $$\\label{eq:all-hit-determinant}\n                         \\Pr(G_I)=\\det(I-K_{BB}).$$ If this probability is positive, then $K_{BB}$ is transient and $$\\label{eq:conditional-next-pin}\n \\mathbb E[\\Sigma\\mid G_I]\n       =K_{II}+K_{IB}(I-K_{BB})^{-1}K_{BI}.$$ The right-hand side is the first positive $I$-hitting kernel of the Markov chain with independent transitions according to $K$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-routed-flow",
        "exact_quote": "Consider an available-slot nearest-neighbor graph on $\\mathbb Z^3$, with at most $\\ell$ slots per site, all pairs of available slots above each nearest-neighbor site edge joined, and all slots available outside a finite set. Suppose $z$ satisfies the two conditions in Definition <a href=\"#def:regular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:regular\">11</a> for this graph, and $R\\ge R_0$. For each available slot $a$ at $y\\in B_R(z)$, there is a flow $F_a$ such that $$\\label{eq:global-flow-envelope}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}F_a=\\mathbf 1_{\\{a\\}},\\qquad\n |F_a(e)|\\le C(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,y))^{-k},\\qquad\n k=2-3\\nu=\\frac{199}{100}.$$ The flows can be chosen with the following common routing construction. For each blocked component $C$, thicken it by supremum radius four, fill the finite nearest-neighbor components of the complement, and call the resulting finite hull $H_C$. Let $\\operatorname{Box}_C^+$ be the unit enlargement of the coordinate bounding box of that thickening. The hull lies in this box. Keep the inclusion-maximal hulls, which are disjoint, and send each original site in such a hull to a chosen surviving exterior-boundary representative, fixing other sites. Apply this map only once to each original endpoint.*\n\n\n*There are simple surviving routes for the original lattice edges. Each route is contained in its original edge together with the boxes $\\operatorname{Box}_C^+$ of the maximal hulls containing its original endpoints. Lift the routes using a fixed available slot at every surviving site. The flow $F_a$ is the sum of a unit path flow along a connector of uniformly bounded length and displacement from $a$, and the routed values of a lattice unit flow $G_y$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:lattice-flow-envelope}\n |G_y(e')|\\le C(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e',y))^{-2}.$$ All constants are uniform over the graph, anchor, radius, and source.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-routed-flow",
          "exact_quote": "Consider an available-slot nearest-neighbor graph on $\\mathbb Z^3$, with at most $\\ell$ slots per site, all pairs of available slots above each nearest-neighbor site edge joined, and all slots available outside a finite set. Suppose $z$ satisfies the two conditions in Definition <a href=\"#def:regular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:regular\">11</a> for this graph, and $R\\ge R_0$. For each available slot $a$ at $y\\in B_R(z)$, there is a flow $F_a$ such that $$\\label{eq:global-flow-envelope}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}F_a=\\mathbf 1_{\\{a\\}},\\qquad\n |F_a(e)|\\le C(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,y))^{-k},\\qquad\n k=2-3\\nu=\\frac{199}{100}.$$ The flows can be chosen with the following common routing construction. For each blocked component $C$, thicken it by supremum radius four, fill the finite nearest-neighbor components of the complement, and call the resulting finite hull $H_C$. Let $\\operatorname{Box}_C^+$ be the unit enlargement of the coordinate bounding box of that thickening. The hull lies in this box. Keep the inclusion-maximal hulls, which are disjoint, and send each original site in such a hull to a chosen surviving exterior-boundary representative, fixing other sites. Apply this map only once to each original endpoint.*\n\n\n*There are simple surviving routes for the original lattice edges. Each route is contained in its original edge together with the boxes $\\operatorname{Box}_C^+$ of the maximal hulls containing its original endpoints. Lift the routes using a fixed available slot at every surviving site. The flow $F_a$ is the sum of a unit path flow along a connector of uniformly bounded length and displacement from $a$, and the routed values of a lattice unit flow $G_y$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:lattice-flow-envelope}\n |G_y(e')|\\le C(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e',y))^{-2}.$$ All constants are uniform over the graph, anchor, radius, and source.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Consider an available-slot nearest-neighbor graph on $\\mathbb Z^3$, with at most $\\ell$ slots per site, all pairs of available slots above each nearest-neighbor site edge joined, and all slots available outside a finite set. Suppose $z$ satisfies the two conditions in Definition <a href=\"#def:regular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:regular\">11</a> for this graph, and $R\\ge R_0$. For each available slot $a$ at $y\\in B_R(z)$, there is a flow $F_a$ such that $$\\label{eq:global-flow-envelope}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}F_a=\\mathbf 1_{\\{a\\}},\\qquad\n |F_a(e)|\\le C(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e,y))^{-k},\\qquad\n k=2-3\\nu=\\frac{199}{100}.$$ The flows can be chosen with the following common routing construction. For each blocked component $C$, thicken it by supremum radius four, fill the finite nearest-neighbor components of the complement, and call the resulting finite hull $H_C$. Let $\\operatorname{Box}_C^+$ be the unit enlargement of the coordinate bounding box of that thickening. The hull lies in this box. Keep the inclusion-maximal hulls, which are disjoint, and send each original site in such a hull to a chosen surviving exterior-boundary representative, fixing other sites. Apply this map only once to each original endpoint.*\n\n\n*There are simple surviving routes for the original lattice edges. Each route is contained in its original edge together with the boxes $\\operatorname{Box}_C^+$ of the maximal hulls containing its original endpoints. Lift the routes using a fixed available slot at every surviving site. The flow $F_a$ is the sum of a unit path flow along a connector of uniformly bounded length and displacement from $a$, and the routed values of a lattice unit flow $G_y$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:lattice-flow-envelope}\n |G_y(e')|\\le C(1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(e',y))^{-2}.$$ All constants are uniform over the graph, anchor, radius, and source.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-sparse-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R\\ge R_0$, and let $(z_i)_{i=1}^m$ be a sparse list of centers, counting repetitions. At a fixed slice let $I\\subseteq\n\\{1,\\ldots,m\\}$ be any subset for which the slice is regular for $z_i$ at radius $R$. For $i\\in I$, let $\\mu_i\\ge0$ be a mass on the available slots, supported above $\\mathfrak B_R(z_i)$ and with total mass at most one. For every finitely supported real $Y$, $$\\label{eq:sparse-source-pairing}\n \\left|\\left\\langle\\sum_{i\\in I}\\mu_i,Y\\right\\rangle\\right|^2\n \\le C m R^\\alpha\\mathcal D_t(Y).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-271.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-spread-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $R\\ge R_0$, $1\\le\\tau\\le R$, and the slice $t$ is regular for $z$ at radius $R$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-blocking",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $\\mathsf I(P)$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-component-tail",
        "exact_quote": "There are $\\beta_0,c,C,\\lambda>0$ such that, if $\\beta\\ge\\beta_0$ and $\\mathsf I(P)$ holds, then $$\\label{eq:component-tail}\n \\mathbb P_P\\bigl(w\\in\\mathcal O_t^\\gamma,\n   \\operatorname{rad}_w\\mathcal C_t^\\gamma(w)\\ge h\\bigr)\n \\le C\\exp(-ch^\\lambda),\\qquad h\\ge1.$$ This holds for every deterministic time $t$, coset $\\gamma$, and coordinate site $w$, with the same constants for all $\\beta\\ge\\beta_0$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-irregularity",
        "exact_quote": "Under $\\mathsf I(P)$, for $\\beta\\ge\\beta_0$, every deterministic time $t$, anchor $z\\in\\mathbb Z^3$, and radius $R\\ge1$ satisfy $$\\label{eq:irregularity}\n \\mathbb P_P(t\\text{ is not regular for }z\\text{ at radius }R)\n \\le\\delta_R:=CR^3p^6+C\\exp(-cR^\\xi),\n \\qquad \\xi=\\nu\\lambda>0.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-localization",
        "exact_quote": "One may choose $a$ in <a href=\"#eq:scales\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scales\">[eq:scales]</a> independently of $K$ so that the following holds, without assuming $\\mathsf I(P)$. Let $i$ be a deterministic slot point that is almost surely a hole under the exposure law for an allowed pin set $P$, and put $z=\\bar x_i$. Let $\\mathcal K$ be a deterministic schedule of at most $2K+2$ full or partial boundary-killed traversals starting at $i$, each in either direction and each covering at most one period. Use matching layers at every concatenation. If $\\mathcal K_R$ is localized throughout at $z$, then $$\\label{eq:localization-loss}\n \\mathbb E_P\\bigl\\|(\\mathcal K-\\mathcal K_R)\n          \\mathbf 1_{\\{i\\}}\\bigr\\|_1\\le C_K e^{-c\\beta}.$$ The same estimate holds for localization of the walk on the full slot lattice, with all slots available and no exposed trajectories.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-localization",
          "exact_quote": "One may choose $a$ in <a href=\"#eq:scales\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scales\">[eq:scales]</a> independently of $K$ so that the following holds, without assuming $\\mathsf I(P)$. Let $i$ be a deterministic slot point that is almost surely a hole under the exposure law for an allowed pin set $P$, and put $z=\\bar x_i$. Let $\\mathcal K$ be a deterministic schedule of at most $2K+2$ full or partial boundary-killed traversals starting at $i$, each in either direction and each covering at most one period. Use matching layers at every concatenation. If $\\mathcal K_R$ is localized throughout at $z$, then $$\\label{eq:localization-loss}\n \\mathbb E_P\\bigl\\|(\\mathcal K-\\mathcal K_R)\n          \\mathbf 1_{\\{i\\}}\\bigr\\|_1\\le C_K e^{-c\\beta}.$$ The same estimate holds for localization of the walk on the full slot lattice, with all slots available and no exposed trajectories.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.1"

      ],
      "exact_statement": "One may choose $a$ in <a href=\"#eq:scales\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scales\">[eq:scales]</a> independently of $K$ so that the following holds, without assuming $\\mathsf I(P)$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-diffusion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an exposure good for $z,R_K$. Let a kernel be a subinterval of a concatenation of full traversals in arbitrary directions, localized throughout at $z$.         "source_id": "PM-271.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-hitting-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be allowed and let $T\\ne\\varnothing$ be a finite set of slot points at a deterministic cut $\\theta$, disjoint from $E$. Put $P=E\\cup T$, $m_T=|T|$, and $\\eta=\\mathbf 1_{T}$. At almost every exposure under $\\mathbb P_P$, define $f=1$ on $T$; at every other cut hole let $f$ be the probability that the boundary-killed fresh walk ever hits $T$ at a subsequent integer turn. Then $0\\le f\\le1$, $f$ has zero boundary values, and, for $g=f-\\eta$, $$\\label{eq:hitting-energy}\n \\sum_{i\\in T}q_i(\\mathbf a)\n \\le m_T-\\langle f,A_Qf\\rangle\n =\\langle\\eta,Q\\eta\\rangle+2\\langle Q\\eta,g\\rangle\n       -\\langle g,A_Qg\\rangle.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-271.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-sparse-pins",
        "exact_quote": "There is $\\beta_1<\\infty$, independent of the cube and pins, such that for every $\\beta\\ge\\beta_1$, every allowed pin set $E$, and every nonempty sparse set $T$ at one deterministic time, disjoint from $E$, $$\\label{eq:sparse-pins}\n \\mathbb P_E(T\\subseteq\\mathcal B_E)\n \\le\\beta^{-9|T|/10}.$$ Consequently <a href=\"#eq:irregularity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:irregularity\">[eq:irregularity]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:good-exposure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:good-exposure\">[eq:good-exposure]</a> hold under every allowed pin law, with no induction hypothesis.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-sparse-pins",

          "source_id": "PM-271.1"

      "exact_statement": "There is $\\beta_1<\\infty$, independent of the cube and pins, such that for every $\\beta\\ge\\beta_1$, every allowed pin set $E$, and every nonempty sparse set $T$ at one deterministic time, disjoint from $E$, $$\\label{eq:sparse-pins}\n \\mathbb P_E(T\\subseteq\\mathcal B_E)\n \\le\\beta^{-9|T|/10}.$$ Consequently <a href=\"#eq:irregularity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:irregularity\">[eq:irregularity]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:good-exposure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:good-exposure\">[eq:good-exposure]</a> hold under every allowed pin law, with no induction hypothesis.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-return-expansion",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-singleton-bound",
        "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large $\\beta$, uniformly in all allowed pin sets $E$, cubes, cuts, and slot-time points $i\\notin E$, $$\\label{eq:singleton-return}\n r(E,\\{i\\})\\le C\\beta^{-3/2}.$$ Consequently, for every allowed pin set $D$ and every slot-time point $j$ at a deterministic time, $$\\label{eq:free-slot}\n \\mathbb P_D(j\\in\\mathcal B_D)\\le C\\beta^{-3/2}.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ideal-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "The full site kernel satisfies $$\\label{eq:ideal-site-bounds}\n \\sup_{y,w}p_t(y,w)\\le C(1+t)^{-3/2},\\qquad\n p_t(x,x)=t^{-3/2}(\\kappa+o(1)).$$ The first estimate holds for all $t\\ge0$; the second is as $t\\to\\infty$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Pressure and spontaneous magnetization** ([source](#eq:pressure-magnetization)): For $\beta>0$ and $h\in\mathbb R$, put $$p_\beta(h)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac1{\beta\|\Lambda_N\|} \log\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-\beta(H^J_{S,N}-hM_{S,N})}, \qquad m_{S,J}(\beta)=\partial_h^+p_\beta(0). \label{eq:pressure-magnetization}$$
- **Quadratic diffusion matrix** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Define the positive definite matrix $$D_{S,J}=\frac S2\sum_{z\in\mathbb Z^3}J(z)zz^{\mathsf T}. \label{eq:diffusion-matrix}$$
- **One-magnon dispersion** ([source](#pm-h-3)): It is the quadratic coefficient of the one-magnon dispersion: $$\varepsilon_{S,J}(k)=S\sum_zJ(z)(1-\cos(k\cdot z)) =k^{\mathsf T}D_{S,J}k+O(\|k\|^4). \label{eq:dispersion}$$
- **Pin** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A *pin* is a specified slot-time point.
- **Allowed pin set** ([source](#pm-h-8)): An *allowed* pin set is $D=D_0\cup F$ with $F$ finite and deterministic.
- **Sparse collection** ([source](#stmt-def-sparse)): A finite collection of slot points with site coordinates $(x_i)$ is *sparse* if $$\label{eq:sparse-growth} \#\{i:\bar x_i\in B_h(z)\}\le L(1+h)^\alpha \qquad(z\in\mathbb Z^3,\ h\ge0).$$ Repeated bar coordinates count with their multiplicities, including points from different cosets. The same definition applies to a list of centers in $\mathbb Z^3$. Every subcollection remains sparse.
- **Regular slice** ([source](#stmt-def-regular)): A slice $t$ is *regular for the anchor $z$ at radius $R$* if the following hold in every coset $\gamma$: 1. for every $w\in B_{11R}(z)$, $\#(\mathcal O_t^\gamma\cap B_{M_*}(w))<6$; 2. for every $w\in\mathcal O_t^\gamma\setminus B_{10R}(z)$, $\operatorname{rad}_w\mathcal C_t^\gamma(w) \le(1+\|w-z\|_\infty)^\nu$. The anchor itself need not be an available site.
- **Fresh walk** ([source](#pm-h-12)): The *fresh walk* follows the one-particle transitions of $\mathbb Q_{\mathbf a}$: it jumps across each available slot edge at rate $c_e$, follows the forced transports, and at a seam uses a fresh uniform residual completion.
- **Full Dirichlet energy** ([source](#eq:norm-dissipation)): For a finitely supported real function $v$ on this graph, define $$\label{eq:full-energy} \mathcal D_t(v)= \sum_{e=\{i,j\}\in E(\mathcal H_t)}c_e\bigl(v(i)-v(j)\bigr)^2, \qquad c_e=\tfrac12 J(x_j-x_i).$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-prop-loop-representation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-loop-representation): Symmetric-slot trace representation. -> Colored exchange-cycle law.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-pin-identities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pin-identities): Weight change and stable-polynomial negative dependence. -> Return formula and simultaneous avoidance bound.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-fresh-walk) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fresh-walk): Exposure disintegration and exterior-moment next-pin kernel. -> Fresh-walk representation of return fractions.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-spread-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-spread-energy): Local Nash estimates and routed-flow near/far splitting. -> Energy pairing bound for spread mass.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-thm-sparse-pins) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-sparse-pins): Downward induction with sparse witnesses and suffix energy. -> Uniform sparse-pin estimate and good exposures.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-long-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-long-tail): Finite return expansion and completion of the energy square. -> Long-return error with truncation-independent leading constant.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-word) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-word): Common-layer midpoint reduction and coupling to an independent ideal path. -> Sharp finite-word comparison.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-thm-return-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-return-bounds): Finite-word comparison and Bonferroni lower bound. -> Sharp upper and lower one-slot bounds.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-pin-mixture) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pin-mixture): Field tilt through extra pins. -> Finite-volume derivative deficit as a pinned-law mixture.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pin-mixture-2): Integrate pressure increments before taking the thermodynamic and field limits, then the low-temperature and truncation limits. -> Bloch asymptotic law.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

More precisely, for every function $\\Phi$ on the finite spectrum of $M_{S,N}$, $$\\label{eq:loop-spectral-law}\n \\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\bigl(e^{-\\beta H^J_{S,N}}\\Phi(M_{S,N})\\bigr)}{Z_\\Lambda(0)}\n   =\\frac{\\mathbb E_{\\rm b}\\sum_\\sigma\\Phi(M(\\sigma))}\n           {\\mathbb E_{\\rm b}2^{\\#\\{\\text{cycles}\\}}}.$$",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **next-pin**: The conditional next-pin first moment agrees with the first positive hitting kernel of independent transitions of the mean permutation matrix.
- **limit-order**: Spontaneous magnetization is obtained after the thermodynamic limit and before the low-temperature limit, with spin and interaction fixed.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **singular-conditioning**: Invert the nonpin resolvent when the all-cycle-hit event has zero probability. -> The conditional permutation law is undefined and the stated transient resolvent is unavailable.
- **arbitrary-permutation-law**: Apply the determinant formula to an arbitrary random permutation with the same mean matrix. -> The mean alone does not establish the required exterior-moment identity.
- **finite-certifies-bloch**: Treat a finite next-pin calculation as a certificate of the spontaneous-magnetization asymptotic. -> A finite calculation does not perform the thermodynamic, zero-field, and low-temperature limits.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Finite permutation products instantiate the conditional next-pin kernel; the thermodynamic asymptotic is outside this computation.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of finite cases {n, pins, swaps, perm}; swaps are [a,b,rational_probability].
# PRECONDITIONS: 1<=n<=3; nonempty distinct pins; at most two swaps; perm is a permutation.
# OUTPUTS: Exact all-cycle-hit probability g and column-forward conditional next-pin matrix H; H=null if g=0.
# TIME: Cubic in n per case for exact matrix operations.
# SPACE: Quadratic in n per case, excluding the input and output batch.
# ARITHMETIC: FLINT exact rational matrices; no floating point or solver witnesses.
# FAILURE: Invalid contracts raise ValueError; zero-probability conditioning returns null without inversion.
import json,sys
from flint import fmpq,fmpq_mat

def solve(c):
    n=c['n']; pins=c['pins']; swaps=c['swaps']; perm=c['perm']
    if not isinstance(n,int) or not 1<=n<=3 or not pins or len(pins)!=len(set(pins)) or any(i not in range(n) for i in pins) or sorted(perm)!=list(range(n)) or len(swaps)>2:
        raise ValueError('invalid finite contract')
    eye=lambda m:fmpq_mat([[int(i==j) for j in range(m)] for i in range(m)])
    K=eye(n)
    for a,b,p in swaps:
        p=fmpq(p)
        if a not in range(n) or b not in range(n) or a==b or not 0<=p<=1: raise ValueError('invalid swap')
        T=eye(n); T[a,a]=1-p; T[b,b]=1-p; T[a,b]=p; T[b,a]=p
        K=T*K
    P=fmpq_mat([[int(i==perm[j]) for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)])
    K=P*K
    B=[j for j in range(n) if j not in pins]
    block=lambda rows,cols:fmpq_mat([[K[i,j] for j in cols] for i in rows])
    H=block(pins,pins)
    if B:
        A=eye(len(B))-block(B,B)
        g=A.det()
        if g==0: return {'g':str(g),'H':None}
        H=H+block(pins,B)*A.inv()*block(B,pins)
    else:
        g=fmpq(1)
    return {'g':str(g),'H':[[str(H[i,j]) for j in range(len(pins))] for i in range(len(pins))]}
print(json.dumps([solve(c) for c in json.load(sys.stdin)],separators=(',',':')))
```

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |

| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Finite next-pin matrix identities can serve as a mathematical contract for a probability routine. A software implementation and its numerical error guarantees are ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Exchange pictures give a finite-volume representation for equilibrium spin simulation. A sampler for the weighted picture law and a convergence certificate are ABSENT. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper supplies mathematical identities and asymptotic bounds. A user-facing tool, interface, or certified numerical package is ABSENT. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The next-pin identity reduces a conditional cycle first moment to finite determinant and linear-system calculations under explicit hypotheses. Complexity guarantees are ABSENT. |
| data_structures | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Finite slot layers, permutation cycles and matrix blocks provide an adaptable representation of the exchange construction. A data-structure performance result is ABSENT. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns lattice spin equilibrium and finite matrix identities; accelerator architecture and parallel execution guarantees are ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The pin and cycle identities concern equilibrium probabilities; a cryptographic primitive, adversary model or security reduction is ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper establishes magnetization and return estimates for a prescribed spin model; a learning objective, estimator guarantee or training method is ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Routed flows bound Dirichlet-energy pairings in the proof. A finite optimisation problem, objective and terminating optimisation procedure are ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Fresh transitions in a repeated exposure describe a Markov construction. A distributed protocol, fault model or consistency guarantee is ABSENT. |
| other (quantum statistical mechanics) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The direct application is low-temperature equilibrium magnetization of quantum Heisenberg ferromagnets under the stated interaction assumptions and order of limits. A finite-temperature error rate is ABSENT. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (hypothesis): The magnetization law concerns fixed positive quantum spin and nonnegative symmetric finite-range interactions generating the lattice. Blocked use: ['Extension to signed, asymmetric, infinite-range, or nongenerating interactions is not established.', 'A joint varying-spin low-temperature limit is not established.']
- **L02** (hypothesis): The thermodynamic limit precedes the right zero-field derivative, and the low-temperature limit follows with the model fixed. Blocked use: ['Do not substitute a finite-volume zero-field derivative or interchange these limits.']
- **L03** (approximation): The full interaction kernel determines the leading coefficient; the chosen sublattice supplies only estimates. Blocked use: ['Do not compute the coefficient using only the selected geometric bonds.']
- **L04** (approximation): The return cutoff is fixed while the low-temperature error vanishes; a rate uniform in the cutoff is not supplied. Blocked use: ['Do not choose a temperature-dependent return cutoff using a claimed uniform error rate.']
- **L05** (edge_case): The lower down-probability bound requires an interior neighborhood free of pins. Blocked use: ['Do not apply the lower bound near the boundary or inside a neighborhood containing extra pins.']
- **L06** (hypothesis): The determinantal next-pin identity requires the exterior-moment property of the permutation law. Blocked use: ['Do not extend the identity to arbitrary random permutation laws solely because their mean matrix is doubly stochastic.']
- **L07** (edge_case): The conditional next-pin kernel requires positive probability that every cycle meets the pins. Blocked use: ['Do not condition on an event of zero probability or invert the nonpin resolvent without its required positivity.']
- **L08** (edge_case): With no nonpins, the all-hit determinant is one and the next-pin correction vanishes. Blocked use: ['Do not require a nonempty nonpin block to implement the finite identity.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Bloch's <i>T</i><sup>3/2</sup> law for the spontaneous magnetization of three-dimensional quantum Heisenberg ferromagnets at every fixed positive quantum spin. The result holds for every nonnegative symmetric finite-range interaction whose support generates ℤ<sup>3</sup>, including spatially anisotropic couplings. The leading magnetization deficit has the exact coefficient determined by the determinant of the quadratic one-magnon dispersion. The thermodynamic limit is taken before the right field derivative at zero, and the low-temperature limit is taken last.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Bloch’s law predicts that the spontaneous magnetization of a three-dimensional ferromagnet falls below its fully polarized value by an amount proportional to $T^{3/2}$ at low temperature. We prove this law for the quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet at every fixed spin, allowing any nonnegative symmetric finite-range interaction whose support generates the lattice. The interaction may be spatially anisotropic and need not contain the ordinary nearest-neighbor bonds. Its quadratic dispersion determines the leading coefficient.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The model and the result

<a id="eq:interaction"></a>
Fix $S\in\{\tfrac12,1,\tfrac32,\ldots\}$. The spin space at each site is $\mathfrak h_S=\mathbb C^{2S+1}$, with orthonormal basis $(e_m)_{m=-S}^S$ and operators $$S^ze_m=me_m,\qquad
 S^+e_m=\sqrt{S(S+1)-m(m+1)}\,e_{m+1}\quad(m<S),\qquad S^+e_S=0.$$ Set $S^-=(S^+)^*$, $S^x=(S^++S^-)/2$, and $S^y=(S^+-S^-)/(2i)$. A subscript specifies the site where an operator acts. Let $J:\mathbb Z^3\to[0,\infty)$ have finite support and satisfy $$J(0)=0,\qquad J(z)=J(-z),\qquad
 \langle z:J(z)>0\rangle_{\mathbb Z}=\mathbb Z^3.
 \label{eq:interaction}$$ On $\Lambda_N=[-N,N]^3\cap\mathbb Z^3$, with free boundary, define <a id="eq:hamiltonian"></a>$$H^J_{S,N}=-\frac12\sum_{x,y\in\Lambda_N}
               J(y-x)\boldsymbol S_x\cdot\boldsymbol S_y,
 \qquad M_{S,N}=\sum_{x\in\Lambda_N}S_x^z.
 \label{eq:hamiltonian}$$ The sum counts each unordered bond twice; the factor $1/2$ removes that duplication. We set the lattice spacing, Boltzmann constant, and $\hbar$ equal to one, so $T=\beta^{-1}$.

<a id="eq:pressure-magnetization"></a>
For $\beta>0$ and $h\in\mathbb R$, put $$p_\beta(h)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac1{\beta|\Lambda_N|}
       \log\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-\beta(H^J_{S,N}-hM_{S,N})},
 \qquad m_{S,J}(\beta)=\partial_h^+p_\beta(0).
 \label{eq:pressure-magnetization}$$ The finite-range pressure limit exists and is convex and $S$-Lipschitz; Section <a href="#sec:pressure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pressure">7</a> recalls the proof. Rotation by $\pi$ about the $x$-axis makes the pressure even, so $m_{S,J}(\beta)\in[0,S]$. The derivative selects the magnetization in a field tending to zero through positive values after the thermodynamic limit.

Define the positive definite matrix <a id="eq:diffusion-matrix"></a>$$D_{S,J}=\frac S2\sum_{z\in\mathbb Z^3}J(z)zz^{\mathsf T}.
 \label{eq:diffusion-matrix}$$ It is the quadratic coefficient of the one-magnon dispersion: <a id="eq:dispersion"></a>$$\varepsilon_{S,J}(k)=S\sum_zJ(z)(1-\cos(k\cdot z))
       =k^{\mathsf T}D_{S,J}k+O(|k|^4).
 \label{eq:dispersion}$$ Positive definiteness follows because the interaction support spans $\mathbb R^3$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:bloch-law"></a>
**Theorem 1** (Bloch’s law). *For every fixed $S\in\{\tfrac12,1,\tfrac32,\ldots\}$ and every interaction satisfying <a href="#eq:interaction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:interaction">[eq:interaction]</a>, $$\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\beta^{3/2}\bigl(S-m_{S,J}(\beta)\bigr)
   =\frac{\zeta(3/2)}{8\pi^{3/2}\sqrt{\det D_{S,J}}}.
 \label{eq:bloch-law}$$*

</div>

For $J(\pm e_j)=1$ and all other couplings zero, $D_{S,J}=SI$; the theorem gives the usual nearest-neighbor coefficient $\zeta(3/2)/(8\pi^{3/2}S^{3/2})$. The order of limits is part of the assertion: first volume tends to infinity at fixed $\beta,h$, then $h\downarrow0$, and finally $\beta\to\infty$, with $S,J$ fixed.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Historical context and dependencies

Bloch derived the $T^{3/2}$ reduction from long-wavelength spin waves <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Bloch1930](#ref-Bloch1930)]. The bosonic representation of Holstein and Primakoff <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[HolsteinPrimakoff1940](#ref-HolsteinPrimakoff1940)] and Dyson’s analysis of spin-wave interactions <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Dyson1956a](#ref-Dyson1956a), [Dyson1956b](#ref-Dyson1956b)] developed the low-temperature expansion. In particular, Dyson identified the interaction contribution to the magnetization at order $T^4$; his estimates of the remaining higher-order terms were not a rigorous fixed-spin proof of the asymptotic law <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Dyson1956b](#ref-Dyson1956b), Section 9]. The leading coefficient in <a href="#eq:bloch-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bloch-law">[eq:bloch-law]</a> is obtained from the low-temperature density of ideal magnons with quadratic energy $k^{\mathsf T}D_{S,J}k$. The issue is to justify this coefficient for the interacting spin system and the field derivative in <a href="#eq:pressure-magnetization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pressure-magnetization">[eq:pressure-magnetization]</a>.

Rigorous ordering theory distinguishes sharply between dimensions and between classical and quantum models. Mermin and Wagner excluded positive-temperature spontaneous magnetic order for the one- and two-dimensional isotropic Heisenberg models under their finite-range hypotheses <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[MerminWagner1966](#ref-MerminWagner1966)]. Infrared bounds proved symmetry breaking for classical models <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[FSS1976](#ref-FSS1976)], and Dyson, Lieb, and Simon established ordering for classes of quantum antiferromagnets <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[DLS1978](#ref-DLS1978)]. These developments left the standard three-dimensional quantum ferromagnet as a separate problem, recorded by Lieb <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Lieb1999](#ref-Lieb1999)] and still discussed as open in recent accounts <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Seiringer2025](#ref-Seiringer2025), [BjornbergRyan2026](#ref-BjornbergRyan2026)]. The distinct companion manuscript <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Ordering](#ref-Ordering), Theorem 1.1] proves low-temperature magnetized equilibrium states for the nearest-neighbor quantum ferromagnet. We use its finite-set algebra and deterministic lattice geometry, with their precise hypotheses stated at the points of use.

The free energy has a different rigorous history. Conlon and Solovej <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[ConlonSolovej1991](#ref-ConlonSolovej1991)] and Tóth <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Toth1993](#ref-Toth1993)] obtained bounds for spin $1/2$. Correggi, Giuliani, and Seiringer proved the leading low-temperature free-energy asymptotic for the nearest-neighbor model on $\mathbb Z^3$ at every fixed spin <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[CGS2015](#ref-CGS2015), Theorem 2.1]. A zero-field free-energy estimate does not by itself control the one-sided field derivative that defines spontaneous magnetization. Our proof therefore estimates the observable through a representation that remains uniform under arbitrary extra up-spin constraints, and only then passes to pressure increments.

The probabilistic representation belongs to the random-interchange approach of Tóth <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Toth1993](#ref-Toth1993)]; the symmetric-slot realization of higher spin is described by Nachtergaele <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Nachtergaele1994](#ref-Nachtergaele1994)]. The pin argument uses the stable-polynomial approach to negative dependence developed by Borcea, Brändén, and Liggett <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[BBL2009](#ref-BBL2009)]. Diffusive smoothing uses the energy method of Nash <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Nash1958](#ref-Nash1958)]. The companion <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Ordering](#ref-Ordering)] provides the particular stable exchange symbols, presence inequality, local lattice inequality, and routed unit flows used below. We prove here the finite-range pin bootstrap, the quantitative long-return bound, and the comparison that recovers the full dispersion.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### How returns determine the coefficient

Represent each spin by $\ell=2S$ spin-$1/2$ *slots*. Independent Poisson exchanges between slots, followed by a uniform permutation of the slots at each site, form directed cycles over a time period of length $\beta$. A cycle has a single up or down color. Constraining all cycles through the boundary and through specified slot-time points to be up defines a *pin law*. If $E$ is its pin set and $i$ is an unpinned slot-time point, a change of weights gives $$d_E(i)=\frac{r(E,\{i\})}{1+r(E,\{i\})},$$ where $d_E(i)$ is its down probability. Under the law with $i$ also pinned, $r(E,\{i\})$ is the probability that the directed line from $i$ returns to $i$ at positive elapsed time before meeting $E$. Section <a href="#sec:pins" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pins">2</a> makes this identity precise and represents $r$ by a walk on the slots left after exposing the cycles missing the augmented pin set. The exposed environment repeats over periods, while the walk uses fresh transition randomness on every traversal.

There are two geometric tasks. For rough estimates, choose three linearly independent interaction vectors. They generate a sublattice of finite index; on each of its cosets, the chosen bonds form a copy of the ordinary cubic lattice. Section <a href="#sec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:geometry">3</a> transfers local Nash and flow estimates to these copies and compares their energies with the full interaction energy. Section <a href="#sec:bootstrap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:bootstrap">4</a> then proves a sparse-pin estimate by downward induction on the pin set. This induction supplies enough regular time for diffusion without assuming a finite-range ordering theorem.

Sharp asymptotics require all interaction bonds. The ideal walk has site jump rates $SJ(z)$, and its heat kernel $p_t^{S,J}$ satisfies $$p_t^{S,J}(x,x)\sim\kappa t^{-3/2},\qquad
 \kappa=(4\pi)^{-3/2}(\det D_{S,J})^{-1/2}.$$ After a full traversal the slot index is uniform, so the ideal $n$-period return probability to a specified slot is $\ell^{-1}p_{n\beta}^{S,J}(x,x)$. Summing over the $\ell$ slots at a site cancels this factor, leaving the finite sums $\kappa\sum_{n\le K}n^{-3/2}$ that approach the Bloch coefficient. The selected sublattice is used only for estimates; the full jump kernel determines this coefficient.

The main analytic difficulty is controlling arbitrarily late returns in the same repeated environment. Section <a href="#sec:returns" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:returns">5</a> truncates a quadratic hitting-probability identity after $K$ periods. A mass already spread for time comparable to $K\beta$ has a flow to infinity with energy at most $C(K\beta)^{-1/2}$. Averaging over one further period gives a remainder bounded by $CK^{-1/2}\beta^{-3/2}+o_K(\beta^{-3/2})$, with $C$ independent of $K$. Its $K=1$ case first improves the exposed-slot density to $O(\beta^{-3/2})$.

This improved density permits the comparison in Section <a href="#sec:ideal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ideal">6</a>. The ideal path rarely encounters an exposed strand over a fixed number of periods. Splitting a return at its midpoint combines that small encounter probability with diffusive smoothing, giving an error $o_K(\beta^{-3/2})$ in each return entry. The comparison retains the same exposed environment in both halves; independence of their environments is unnecessary. The finite-return comparison and the long-return remainder give matching upper and lower down-probability bounds, with the lower bound requiring a pin-free neighborhood of the starting point.

Finally, Section <a href="#sec:pressure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pressure">7</a> expresses a positive field as a mixture of extra pins. The probability of selecting any given slot is at most $1-e^{-\beta h}$, so the required neighborhood is free of extra pins with probability tending to one as $h\downarrow0$ at fixed $\beta,K$. Integrating the finite-volume derivative bounds before taking the thermodynamic limit proves <a href="#eq:bloch-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bloch-law">[eq:bloch-law]</a> in the stated order. The uniform pin estimates, the finite-index geometric reduction, and the energy bound for a spread source are the reusable parts of the argument.

<a id="sec:pins"></a>
### Exchange pictures and pinned returns

We express the magnetization through colored exchange cycles, then turn pinning probabilities into one-particle return probabilities. Two changes of law are involved. Pinning removes the color factor from cycles that meet the pins. Exposing the cycles that avoid the pins leaves a conditional exchange process on the remaining slots. The last part of this section identifies its first return moment with an unconditioned Markov walk in the exposed environment. These constructions are finite-dimensional; the spatial estimates begin in Section <a href="#sec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:geometry">3</a>.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Spin slots and the exchange law

Fix $\beta>0$ and a cube $\Lambda=\Lambda_N$. Set $\ell=2S$ and $$b_0=\max\{|z|_\infty:J(z)>0\},\qquad
 \partial\Lambda=\{x\in\Lambda:
       \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_\infty(x,\mathbb Z^3\setminus\Lambda)\le b_0\},\qquad
 \Lambda^\circ=\Lambda\setminus\partial\Lambda.$$ No interaction bond joins $\Lambda^\circ$ to the exterior of $\Lambda$. The *slots* are the elements of $V_\Lambda=\Lambda\times\{1,\ldots,\ell\}$. Above each unordered bond $\{x,y\}\subset\Lambda$ with $J(y-x)>0$, include all $\ell^2$ unordered slot pairs. Write $\mathcal E_\Lambda$ for the resulting slot edges and set $$J_e=J(y-x),\qquad c_e=\frac{J_e}{2}
       \quad(e\text{ above }\{x,y\}),\qquad
 w_\Lambda=\sum_{e\in\mathcal E_\Lambda}J_e.$$

Under the *base law* $\mathbb P_{\rm b}$, every slot edge $e$ carries an independent Poisson process of rate $c_e$ on $[0,\beta)$. At a mark the two slot lines exchange. At the end of the interval, independent uniform permutations of the slots at each site join time $\beta$ to time $0$. We call these within-site permutations the *seam completions*. A picture $\omega$ consists of its marks and completions. Following its lines through repeated copies of the same picture gives directed cycles. Their intersections with the time-zero layer are the cycles of a permutation of $V_\Lambda$.

Time belongs to $\mathbb R/\beta\mathbb Z$. At an exchange we use the slot just after that exchange, and at time zero we use the slot just after the seam completion. Deterministic times almost surely avoid Poisson marks. If $a_C$ is the number of time-zero slots on a cycle $C$, then $\sum_Ca_C=\ell|\Lambda|$. A cycle coloring is a choice $\sigma_C\in\{-1,1\}$ for each cycle; its magnetization is $$M(\sigma)=\frac12\sum_Ca_C\sigma_C.$$

<a id="stmt-prop-loop-representation"></a>
<div id="prop:loop-representation" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:loop-partition"></a>
**Proposition 2** (Loop representation). *For every finite cube, every $\beta>0$, and every real $h$, $$\label{eq:loop-partition}
 Z_\Lambda(h):=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-\beta(H^J_{S,N}-hM_{S,N})}
   =e^{\beta w_\Lambda/4}\,
           \mathbb E_{\rm b}\sum_\sigma e^{\beta hM(\sigma)}.$$ At zero field the Gibbs spectral law of total magnetization is obtained by weighting each picture by $2^{\#\{\text{cycles}\}}$, coloring its cycles independently and fairly, and evaluating $M(\sigma)$. More precisely, for every function $\Phi$ on the finite spectrum of $M_{S,N}$, <a id="eq:loop-spectral-law"></a>$$\label{eq:loop-spectral-law}
 \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\bigl(e^{-\beta H^J_{S,N}}\Phi(M_{S,N})\bigr)}{Z_\Lambda(0)}
   =\frac{\mathbb E_{\rm b}\sum_\sigma\Phi(M(\sigma))}
           {\mathbb E_{\rm b}2^{\#\{\text{cycles}\}}}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-loop-representation"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-loop-representation</summary>

*Proof.* This is the exchange representation of Tóth <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Toth1993](#ref-Toth1993)], with the higher-spin symmetric-slot construction of Nachtergaele <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Nachtergaele1994](#ref-Nachtergaele1994), Section 2.2]; see also <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Ordering](#ref-Ordering), Lemma 2.1]. At one site let $\psi_k$ be the normalized sum of the elementary spin-$\tfrac12$ tensors having $k$ up spins, $0\le k\le\ell$. The sum of the slot $z$-spins acts on $\psi_k$ by $k-\ell/2$, and the sum of the raising operators acts by $\sqrt{(\ell-k)(k+1)}\,\psi_{k+1}$. These are the spin-$S$ matrices under $k=S+m$. Thus the physical space is the range of $$\mathcal S_\Lambda=\prod_{x\in\Lambda}\mathcal S_x,
 \qquad \mathcal S_x=\frac1{\ell!}
                  \sum_{\kappa\in\mathfrak S_\ell}\mathcal U_{\kappa,x},$$ where $\mathcal U$ denotes tensor permutation on the slot space.

The spin-$\tfrac12$ identity $\boldsymbol s_i\cdot\boldsymbol s_j
 =\frac12\mathcal U_{(ij)}-\frac14I$ gives the lifted Hamiltonian $$\widehat H=-\frac12\sum_eJ_e\mathcal U_e
                      +\frac14w_\Lambda I,
 \qquad \widehat M=\sum_{i\in V_\Lambda}s_i^z.$$ The full sum over all slot pairs above each bond commutes with every within-site permutation, and hence with $\mathcal S_\Lambda$. The Poisson product $\varphi$ of the exchanges satisfies $$\mathbb E_{\rm b}\mathcal U_\varphi
   =\exp\left\{\beta\sum_ec_e(\mathcal U_e-I)\right\}
   =e^{-\beta w_\Lambda/4}e^{-\beta\widehat H}.$$ For example, the first equality follows from the matrix evolution with generator $\sum_ec_e(\mathcal U_e-I)$; no commutation of individual transpositions is needed. Averaging the seam completions inserts $\mathcal S_\Lambda$. Taking the trace after multiplication by $e^{\beta h\widehat M}$ proves <a href="#eq:loop-partition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:loop-partition">[eq:loop-partition]</a>: in the elementary up/down basis, a tensor permutation has a nonzero diagonal entry exactly when the coloring is constant on each cycle. The same calculation with any function of $\widehat M$ proves the spectral-law assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Pins, boundary approximation, and mark counts

<a id="eq:down-free-cycle"></a>
A *pin* is a specified slot-time point. Define the continuous boundary pin set $$D_0=\partial\Lambda\times\{1,\ldots,\ell\}
                                   \times(\mathbb R/\beta\mathbb Z).$$ An *allowed* pin set is $D=D_0\cup F$ with $F$ finite and deterministic. We also use finite pin sets before taking the boundary limit. For either type of set, put $$n_D(\omega)=\#\{C:C\cap D=\varnothing\},\qquad
 \mathcal B_D(\omega)=\bigcup_{C:\,C\cap D=\varnothing}C,
 \qquad \mathcal Z_D=\mathbb E_{\rm b}2^{n_D},$$ and define <a id="eq:pinned-law"></a>$$\label{eq:pinned-law}
 \mathbb P_D(d\omega)=\mathcal Z_D^{-1}2^{n_D(\omega)}
                                        \mathbb P_{\rm b}(d\omega).$$ The normalizer obeys $1\le\mathcal Z_D\le2^{|V_\Lambda|}$. In the associated zero-field color law, cycles meeting $D$ are up and the remaining cycles receive independent fair colors. Denote by $d_D(i)$ the probability that a slot-time point $i$ is down. Then $$\label{eq:down-free-cycle}
                  2d_D(i)=\mathbb P_D(i\in\mathcal B_D).$$

<a id="stmt-lem-dense-boundary"></a>
<div id="lem:dense-boundary" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Dense boundary cuts). *Let $F$ be a finite deterministic pin set and let $\mathcal T_n$ be increasing finite sets of times containing zero, with dense union. Set $$D_n=F\cup\bigl(\partial\Lambda\times\{1,\ldots,\ell\}
                                      \times\mathcal T_n\bigr),
 \qquad D=F\cup D_0.$$ Almost surely, $n_{D_n}=n_D$ and $\mathcal B_{D_n}=\mathcal B_D$ for all sufficiently large $n$. Moreover $\mathbb P_{D_n}$ converges to $\mathbb P_D$ in total variation. If bounded observables $X_n$ converge almost surely to $X$, with a common deterministic bound, then $\mathbb E_{D_n}X_n\to\mathbb E_DX$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-dense-boundary"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-dense-boundary</summary>

*Proof.* There are finitely many marks per period, their times are distinct, and seam completions preserve sites. Every visit to the boundary therefore contains a time interval of positive length and is detected by a cut from the dense union. There are finitely many cycles, so one finite cut set eventually detects every boundary-touching cycle. The asserted eventual equalities follow. Since $1\le2^{n_{D_n}}\le2^{|V_\Lambda|}$, bounded convergence applies both to the weights and their positive normalizers. This gives convergence of the densities in $L^1(\mathbb P_{\rm b})$, and also the assertion about observables. This argument is at fixed volume. ◻

</details>

Under a pin law the marks need not be independent. The following uniform estimate on lists of distinct marks will suffice for all path counts. Let $\omega_{\rm m}$ be the mark counting measure on $X_\Lambda=\mathcal E_\Lambda\times[0,\beta)$, and write $$\lambda_{\rm b}(\{e\}\times dt)=c_e\,dt.$$

<a id="stmt-lem-factorial"></a>
<div id="lem:factorial" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:factorial-bound"></a>
**Lemma 4** (Factorial domination). *For an allowed or finite pin set $D$, an integer $j\ge1$, and a nonnegative measurable function $F$ on $X_\Lambda^j$, $$\label{eq:factorial-bound}
 \mathbb E_D\sum_{(u_1,\ldots,u_j)\in(\omega_{\rm m})^j_{\ne}}
                         F(u_1,\ldots,u_j)
 \le 2^j\int F\,d\lambda_{\rm b}^{\otimes j}.$$ Here the sum is over ordered tuples of distinct marks. The estimate also holds with any prescribed time order or time restrictions.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-factorial"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-factorial</summary>

*Proof.* Inserting a transposition cuts and rejoins two strands. It joins two cycles or splits one cycle into two, leaving the other cycles unchanged, and preserves the union of the affected strands and their pin incidences. Joining cycles cannot increase $n_D$. On splitting, a cycle avoiding $D$ produces two such cycles, while a cycle meeting $D$ has at least one descendant that still meets $D$. Thus $n_D$ increases by at most one, and the weight increases by at most two. This statement applies outside the null set of coincident marks or deterministic pin times, and also to continuous boundary pins.

The Poisson insertion identity, with the seam fixed, is $$\begin{aligned}
 &\mathbb E_{\rm b}\left[2^{n_D(\omega)}
     \sum_{(u_1,\ldots,u_j)\in(\omega_{\rm m})^j_{\ne}}F(u_1,\ldots,u_j)
                                                        \right]\\
 &\qquad=\int F(u_1,\ldots,u_j)\,
       \mathbb E_{\rm b}2^{n_D(\omega+\sum_{a=1}^j\delta_{u_a})}
                          \prod_{a=1}^j\lambda_{\rm b}(du_a).
\end{aligned}$$ Indeed, in the Poisson series the $k$-mark term has $k!/(k-j)!$ ordered choices for the distinguished marks. Cancelling that factor against $1/k!$ and summing the remaining marks proves the identity. Nonnegativity justifies it even for infinite integrals. The insertion bound and division by $\mathcal Z_D$ prove <a href="#eq:factorial-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:factorial-bound">[eq:factorial-bound]</a>. ◻

</details>

We shall freely use the weaker constant $4^j$ in <a href="#eq:factorial-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:factorial-bound">[eq:factorial-bound]</a>. If an independent fresh Poisson family is sampled as well, a mixed list obeys the product of these bounds: each actual mark contributes $4\lambda_{\rm b}$ and each fresh mark contributes $\lambda_{\rm b}$. This follows by independence and the two factorial measures. It is an unconditional statement under $\mathbb P_D$, which is the law used when counting paths below.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Single and simultaneous pin tests

Let $E$ be allowed or finite, and let $T$ be a nonempty set of $m_T=|T|$ distinct slot points at one deterministic time $\theta$, disjoint from $E$. Set $P=E\cup T$. Repeating a sampled picture, let $\mathcal R_i(E,T)$ be the event that the line from $i\in T$ visits $T$ at strictly positive elapsed time before visiting $E$. Define <a id="eq:pin-return-fraction"></a>$$\label{eq:pin-return-fraction}
 r(E,T)=\frac1{m_T}\sum_{i\in T}
                    \mathbb P_P\bigl(\mathcal R_i(E,T)\bigr).$$ Pinning $T$ changes the picture law in this definition. The next proposition compares the changed law with the probability that $T$ lies on cycles avoiding the original pins.

<a id="stmt-prop-pin-identities"></a>
<div id="prop:pin-identities" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5** (Pin identities). *For $E,T$ as above, <a id="eq:pin-identities"></a>$$\label{eq:pin-identities}
 d_E(i)=\frac{r(E,\{i\})}{1+r(E,\{i\})}\quad(i\notin E),
 \qquad
 \mathbb P_E(T\subseteq\mathcal B_E)\le(2r(E,T))^{m_T}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-pin-identities"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-pin-identities</summary>

*Proof.* For $T=\{i\}$, write $r=r(E,\{i\})$ and $A=\{i\in\mathcal B_E\}$. A line returns to $i$ before $E$ exactly on $A$, so $r(E,\{i\})=\mathbb P_P(A)$. Moreover $2^{n_E}=2^{n_P}(1+\mathbf 1_{A})$, giving $\mathcal Z_E=\mathcal Z_P(1+r)$ and $\mathbb P_E(A)=2r/(1+r)$. Equation <a href="#eq:down-free-cycle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:down-free-cycle">[eq:down-free-cycle]</a> proves the singleton identity. This calculation applies directly to finite and allowed pin sets.

For the simultaneous bound, we use three precise finite-set facts from <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Ordering](#ref-Ordering), Lemmas 3.2, 3.4, and 3.6]. A nonzero polynomial is *stable* if it has no zero when every variable has positive imaginary part. The facts are as follows.

1.  Variable identification preserves stability. Partial derivatives, real specialization, and coefficientwise limits of uniformly bounded degree preserve stability up to the zero polynomial. In particular, after identifying two variables as $t$, taking the coefficient of $t$ preserves stability up to zero.

2.  If a permutation $\pi$ of a finite set $V$ is a product of independent Bernoulli transpositions and deterministic permutations, then $$\mathbb E\prod_{j\in V}(x_j+y_{\pi(j)})$$ is stable. This also holds for independent products of finite-time Poisson exchanges with arbitrary nonnegative rates and uniform permutations on specified subsets.

3.  If a random subset $A$ of a finite set has a stable generating polynomial $\mathbb E\prod_{j\in A}y_j$, then for every subset $B$, $$\Pr(B\subseteq A)\le\prod_{j\in B}\Pr(j\in A).$$

These facts belong to the stable-polynomial method for negative dependence developed in <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[BBL2009](#ref-BBL2009)]. They concern arbitrary finite sets, with no spatial hypothesis. To check the continuous-time scope in (ii), subdivide a finite interval into small slices and replace each edge clock in each slice by its independent indicator of at least one mark.
